=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given N linear functions f_1, f_2, \ldots, f_N, where f_i(x) = A_i x + B_i.
Find the maximum possible value of f_{p_1}(f_{p_2}(\ldots f_{p_K}(1) \ldots )) for a sequence p = (p_1, p_2, \ldots, p_K) of K distinct integers between 1 and N, inclusive.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N K
A_1 B_1
A_2 B_2
\vdots
A_N B_N

Output

Print the answer as an integer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^{5}
- 1 \leq K \leq \text{min}(N,10)
- 1 \leq A_i, B_i \leq 50 (1 \leq i \leq N)
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 2
2 3
1 5
4 2

Sample Output 1

26

Here are all possible p and the corresponding values of f_{p_1}(f_{p_2}(1)):

- p= ( 1,2 ) : f_1(f_2(1))=15
- p= ( 1,3 ) : f_1(f_3(1))=15
- p= ( 2,1 ) : f_2(f_1(1))=10
- p= ( 2,3 ) : f_2(f_3(1))=11
- p= ( 3,1 ) : f_3(f_1(1))=22
- p= ( 3,2 ) : f_3(f_2(1))=26

Therefore, print 26.

Sample Input 2

10 3
48 40
34 22
24 37
45 40
48 31
49 44
45 40
44 6
35 22
39 28

Sample Output 2

216223

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

def cmp(x,y):
    lhs = (x[0]-1)*y[1]
    rhs = (y[0]-1)*x[1]
    if lhs > rhs: return -1 # x before y (higher key)
    if lhs < rhs: return 1
    return 0

Implementation:

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N = data[0]; K = data[1]
    funcs = []
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(N):
        A = data[idx]; B = data[idx+1]; idx += 2
        funcs.append((A,B))
    # sort by increasing (A-1)/B (inner to outer)
    funcs.sort(key=lambda x: (x[0]-1) / x[1])
    NEG = -10**30
    dp = [NEG]*(K+1)
    dp[0] = 1
    for A,B in funcs:
        # choose current as outermost among selected
        # iterate descending
        for t in range(K, 0, -1):
            if dp[t-1] != NEG:
                val = A * dp[t-1] + B
                if val > dp[t]:
                    dp[t] = val
    print(dp[K])

if __name__ == "__main__": main()

Let's analyze exact rank precompute more.

```
from functools import cmp_to_key

def cmp(x,y):
    # x before y if key_x < key_y (ascending)
    lhs = (x[0]-1) * y[1]
    rhs = (y[0]-1) * x[1]
    if lhs < rhs: return -1
    if lhs > rhs: return 1
    return 0

pairs = [(a,b) for a in range(1,51) for b in range(1,51)]
pairs.sort(key=cmp_to_key(cmp))
rank = {p:i for i,p in enumerate(pairs)}
...
funcs.sort(key=lambda x: rank[x])
```

Now, if we sort pairs by cmp, for equal key cmp returns 0. Python sort stable, so order based on generation (A ascending, B ascending). Fine.

Then `funcs.sort(key=lambda x: rank[x])`. Since rank is int, sort ascending by key. Good.

Now, let's consider if the sorted order should be by `(A_i - 1) / B_i` or maybe `A_i / B_i`? Let's re-derive from another perspective to avoid sign error.

We want maximize composition. For two functions f,g. Suppose we have inner value x (result of functions inside). Compare f(g(x)) vs g(f(x)).
f(g(x)) = A_f (A_g x + B_g) + B_f = A_f A_g x + A_f B_g + B_f.
g(f(x)) = A_g A_f x + A_g B_f + B_g.
Difference = A_f B_g + B_f - A_g B_f - B_g = B_g(A_f -1) - B_f(A_g -1).
So f(g) > g(f) iff B_g(A_f -1) > B_f(A_g -1) iff (A_f -1)/B_f > (A_g -1)/B_g. Thus f outer, g inner better if f key > g key. Therefore inner-to-outer order is increasing key. Yes.

f1 dp1=101

Let's write final code:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N, K = data[0], data[1]
    funcs = []
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(N):
        A = data[idx]
        B = data[idx+1]
        idx += 2
        funcs.append((A, B))

    # In an optimal composition, functions are ordered from inner to outer
    # by increasing (A-1)/B.
    funcs.sort(key=lambda x: (x[0] - 1) / x[1])

    dp = [-1] * (K + 1)
    dp[0] = 1

    for A, B in funcs:
        for t in range(K, 0, -1):
            if dp[t - 1] != -1:
                val = A * dp[t - 1] + B
                if val > dp[t]:
                    dp[t] = val

    print(dp[K])

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

```
    data=...
```

Very concise.

Rank version:
```
from functools import cmp_to_key
...
def cmp(x,y):
    lhs=(x[0]-1)*y[1]
    rhs=(y[0]-1)*x[1]
    return (lhs>rhs) - (lhs<rhs)
pairs=[(a,b) for a in range(1,51) for b in range(1,51)]
pairs.sort(key=cmp_to_key(cmp))
rank={p:i for i,p in enumerate(pairs)}
funcs.sort(key=lambda x: rank[x])
```
This is exact but more code. The final answer should be accepted either way. Since the problem constraints are small, float is safe. I think using float is acceptable.
```
def cmp(x, y):
    lhs = (x[0] - 1) * y[1]
    rhs = (y[0] - 1) * x[1]
    if lhs < rhs:
        return -1
    if lhs > rhs:
        return 1
    return 0
```
This compares key_x < key_y? Let's check: key = (A-1)/B. key_x < key_y iff (A_x-1)/B_x < (A_y-1)/B_y iff (A_x-1)*B_y < (A_y-1)*B_x. So lhs = (x[0]-1)*y[1], rhs = (y[0]-1)*x[1]. If lhs < rhs, x key < y key, return -1. Good.
Then `pairs.sort(key=cmp_to_key(cmp))`.
Then `rank = {p: i for i, p in enumerate(pairs)}`.
Then `funcs.sort(key=lambda x: rank[x])`.
This sorts funcs by ascending key. Good.
But note: `pairs` includes all (A,B) with A=1..50, B=1..50. If input has A=50,B=50, rank exists. Good.
This exact approach avoids float entirely. It also avoids sorting N functions by float; sorts by int rank. The key function `lambda x: rank[x]` uses tuple x as key; since x is a tuple (A,B), rank[x] works. Good.

Now, let's decide final code. I think float key version is acceptable. But let's maybe include exact rank precompute to be 100% mathematically sound and avoid any potential criticism. The final answer code with rank precompute:

```python
import sys
from functools import cmp_to_key

def cmp_key(x, y):
    lhs = (x[0] - 1) * y[1]
    rhs = (y[0] - 1) * x[1]
    if lhs < rhs:
        return -1
    if lhs > rhs:
        return 1
    return 0

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, K = data[0], data[1]
    funcs = []
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(N):
        A = data[idx]
        B = data[idx+1]
        idx += 2
        funcs.append((A, B))

    # Exact rank for all possible (A,B) by increasing (A-1)/B.
    pairs = [(a, b) for a in range(1, 51) for b in range(1, 51)]
    pairs.sort(key=cmp_to_key(cmp_key))
    rank = {p: i for i, p in enumerate(pairs)}

    funcs.sort(key=lambda x: rank[x])

    dp = [-1] * (K + 1)
    dp[0] = 1
    for A, B in funcs:
        for t in range(K, 0, -1):
            if dp[t - 1] != -1:
                val = A * dp[t - 1] + B
                if val > dp[t]:
                    dp[t] = val
    print(dp[K])

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is exact. But there is a subtle issue: `pairs.sort(key=cmp_to_key(cmp_key))` with cmp_key comparing by key ascending. If two pairs have equal key, cmp returns 0. Python sort stable. lhs=(1-1)*2=0, rhs=(4-1)*5=15. lhs<rhs -> -1, so x before y. Good.
For y,x: lhs=(4-1)*5=15, rhs=(1-1)*2=0, lhs>rhs -> 1, so y after x. Good.
So comparator correct.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the final code if we use `func
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N, K = data[0], data[1]
    funcs = []
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(N):
        A = data[idx]
        B = data[idx + 1]
        idx += 2
        funcs.append((A, B))

    # Optimal order is: inner -> outer by increasing (A-1)/B.
    funcs.sort(key=lambda x: (x[0] - 1) / x[1])

    dp = [-1] * (K + 1)
    dp[0] = 1

    for A, B in funcs:
        for t in range(K, 0, -1):
            if dp[t - 1] != -1:
                val = A * dp[t - 1] + B
                if val > dp[t]:
                    dp[t] = val

    print(dp[K])

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
